`a7a5` - Queen on f5 cannot take a5.
`a7a6` - Queen on f5 cannot take a6.

What about `f7f6`?
It's a slow move.

What about `g7f6`?
It moves the bishop and blocks the f-file.

Let's go back to `e8g8`.
Castling is almost always a good move in this kind of position.
Is there any immediate threat if I castle?
The White Queen is on f5. The White Bishop is on c3.
If King is on g8:
- Queen on f5 cannot check it immediately.
- Bishop on c3 is on the a1-h8 diagonal. King on g8 is safe.

So, `e8g8` is the best move among the available options.

`e8g8` is listed.
`h8g8` is also listed.

Some interfaces just use the king move.
Let's verify if there are any other better moves.
`g7f6` - develops the bishop's influence.
But `e8g8` is just fundamentally better for the king's safety.

Wait, I should check for any White threats I missed.
White Queen f5, White Bishop c3.

Wait, if White has no pawns on c2, d2, e2, they might have moved them.
This suggests the center is open, making castling even more urgent.

I already checked: `g7b2` is captured by `c3b2`.
What about `g7f6`?
It doesn't seem to accomplish much.
